### Pricing Strategies

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IPricing StrategiesThe following are four different pricing strategies that will help guide the entrepreneur pricing strategy in a simple way:
Economy Pricing: Low priced, affordable pricing for everyone – Profit margins in this pricing category are small. This pricing strategy is designed to target a mass, broad market. In a service industry, this pricing strategy is typically reserved for large corporations.

Skimming Pricing: This pricing strategy charges a higher price at the start of the company and eventually lowers the pricing as more competitors enter the market. This strategy is typically used for companies entering a new market or market segment that is not very competitive.

Penetration Pricing: This strategy sets pricing lower at the start of the company in order to gain a share of the market quickly. Once the business has met its market share goals, prices are raised to a higher level. This strategy also allows for feedback from customers and helps with the decision on where a valid price point should be set.

Premium Pricing: Setting a high price is used to define the product or brand as a luxury item. In the mind of the consumer, the high price must mean a high-quality product. This strategy is used by high-end brands in the automotive and luxury watch industries.
Quality of Product and PricingQuality of Product
Price
Low
High
Low
Economy
Penetration
High
Skimming
Premium
Note that the entrepreneur may pivot at different points in time to different pricing strategies based on sales and feedback from customers. However, ensure that the business plan is updated to consider the new pricing strategy.

Whichever pricing model is chosen, price according to the target market. If the target market is college students, the pricing will need to be at the lower end of the market. If the target market is celebrities, then pricing will need to be at the higher end of the market.
Business Plan Questions
    Will the service best fit into an economy, penetration, skimming, or premium price strategy?
    Will the company charge a one-time, pre-paid package fee or a recurring monthly subscription?
    Will the company test other pricing strategies or price points?

Executive Fitness Example1. Will the service best fit into an economy, penetration, skimming, or premium price strategy?

The long-term goal of the service is to be priced as a premium service. To start the company and ensure that service quality commands a premium price point, services will be priced in the middle of the market, in a hybrid of penetration and premium pricing.

2. Will the company charge a one-time, pre-paid package fee or a recurring monthly subscription?

The product will be a recurring monthly subscription with a 3-month minimum subscription required. This will help to keep the cost lower, but still offer premium pricing. This will also allow the company to have a more predictable revenue stream.

3. Will the company test other pricing strategies or price points?

Utilizing feedback from clients and comparing that with projections and goals, the company will adjust pricing accordingly at 3-month intervals in the first year of business.

---

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IContinuing with the example company, because research showed there is a wide range of prices in the market, it is time to discover where the example company falls in the market. For this, a simple solution is to use a hybrid of the premium and penetration pricing models and be prepared to adjust based on feedback from clients. Setting a price around the average market price of $243 and deciding on $249 will put the business into a prime position for the average consumer. (Using an odd number for pricing is called psychological pricing—there is more resistance to $250 versus $249).
Helpful HintDo not change prices too often. Re-evaluate pricing every 3 months in the first year of business, every 6 months in the second year, and then yearly thereafter. Also, consider inflation in the economy. The average inflation rate is 3%, which means that prices will double about every 20 years. If prices are set at $100 for 10 years and that price is suddenly increased to $150 to offset inflation, many customers will be unhappy. However, a smart entrepreneur will plan for price increases. A good rule of thumb is a 5% price increase every other year or 10% every 3 years. This will help prevent a pricing shock to customers and a decline in purchasing power over time.
The example company now has a price of $249 for the coaching product, but still has three products. Pricing all three at the same rate would not give customers a price choice and makes it harder for the consumer to differentiate between products. $249 can be used for the base price or the upgrade price. The example company will use $249 as the upgrade and $199 for the base price, as a 25% upgrade price ($50 in this example) is a substantial revenue increase for the business, and a fair price for the client. Refer to Table: Base Price and Upgrade Price Example for the pricing options.
Base Price and Upgrade Price ExampleOnline Exercise Coaching
Online Nutrition Coaching
Complete Online Fitness Coaching
$199/month
$199/month
$249/month

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IThe company now has three products, each with their own pricing and the ability for clients to upgrade or downgrade. Downgrading is an important option to provide clients. It is better to have a client downgrade rather than leave altogether. Using these tools, the entrepreneur can create their own product lineup and pricing models that allow for products to be priced competitively in the market, upsell useful services to clients, and retain clients with different services that meet a client’s changing needs.
Helpful Hint
    Add-ons can either cost more than the original product and require less sales or can cost less but will require more sales.
    
        Cost more: less products that need to be created
        Cost less: will need new products more often to keep small amounts of money coming in
    
    



    After operating for 6 months, the entrepreneur should re-evaluate services that are selling and see if they can find where most of their revenue comes from.
    
        For example, if a business has 80% of their sales coming from 20% of their products (referred to as the Pareto Principle or the 80/20 principle), drop products that do not sell and/or do not generate enough revenue to warrant continued development.
    
    

Food for ThoughtTwo common pricing options include having many clients who pay a little or a few clients who pay a lot. Having a few clients who pay more typically means there will be fewer daily headaches. It is easier to communicate with 10 people versus 100. The tradeoff for that is if two clients leave, the business will lose 20% of its income. One of the important factors of pricing is to find a balance between workload and revenue security.

### Introduction to a Virtual Coaching Business Plan

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IIntroduction to a Virtual Coaching Business PlanA business plan is the instruction manual for how the business will grow, which outlines the path the fitness professional will follow to achieve their overall vision. As an instruction manual, a business plan is a living document that must be reviewed, updated, and rewritten as a business evolves and changes. Having a business plan gives the fitness professional a place to put their thoughts into words and gives them the ability to easily share their vision with others.
The length and detail of the fitness professional’s business plan can and will vary depending on several factors. If the new entrepreneur is seeking to take out a bank loan or take on partners or investors, they will need to go into greater detail on financial information than they would if they were starting a business on their own. If the business owner is a solopreneur (an entrepreneur with no employees or partners), then the goal of the business plan is to have a guide to keep the fitness professional on course while building their business.
Please note: After each description of the different sections of the business plan are at least three Business Plan Questions to help the fitness professional develop their own well-thought-out and detailed business plan. These prompts will be followed by an example of properly developed answers that will be used to formulate a final business plan, which will be completed at the end of this chapter.
An example company, called Executive Fitness, will be created to demonstrate what well-thought-out answers to the business plan writing prompts should look like. The example company’s goal is to provide online fitness coaching to busy office professionals who do not have time to visit a gym on a consistent basis. The example company will be expanded on as the business plan is developed further in this chapter.
The Business Plan Questions should be completed after reading the entire chapter because many of these questions and ideas work and build off of each other. In order to develop a complete picture of what the fitness professional’s virtual coaching business will look like, the fitness professional must understand all of the concepts in this chapter first.
Food for ThoughtA business plan should be treated as a living document. It should be reviewed, referenced, and updated regularly.
Business planA document that outlines how a business will be structured, grow, and achieve goals that are set forth by the business owner or executive team.
SolopreneurAn entrepreneur who runs a business on their own.

---

### Executive Summary

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IExecutive SummaryZurainy Zain/Shutterstock.comThe first section of a business plan is the executive summary. In the executive summary, the fitness professional will describe the purpose of the business. This part is the who, what, and why of the business.

    Who are the founders and key people in the business? Who is the business targeting? What other stakeholders should be considered?
    What makes this business different, more effective, and, most importantly, unique?
    Why are clients going to come to this new, untested, and unproven business?

Each section of the executive summary can expand over time into their own sections of the business plan as the fitness professional develops more experience and a more detailed path for bringing their overall vision for the business to fruition.
Mission and Vision StatementsAs an entrepreneur, the fitness professional is the business’s management team. The fitness professional is, essentially, the director of everything. This is where, as the company’s chief visionary, they will lay the foundation for the future of the company for years to come. The fitness professional should discuss where they see the business in 1, 5, and 10 years along with the milestones of how they plan to get to each of those goals. They will discuss the driving force behind their business and their company's vision and mission statement.
Tang Yan Song/Shutterstock.comA mission statement is what drives the business’s culture and progress over time. A mission statement is the entrepreneur’s reason for wanting to start this business and should convey a meaning that resonates (and motivates) with the founder, employees, and clients (Bart, 1997).

A vision statement is what the business will look like in the long-term, over the next 5 to 10 years or more. If the overall goals of the business are met, the vision statement should describe an outside, broad view of the company.
Executive summaryA section of the business plan that provides an overview of what will be covered in the document.
Mission statementA mission statement is the entrepreneur’s reason for wanting to start this business and should convey a meaning that resonates (and motivates) with the founder, employees, and clients.
Vision statementWhat the business will look like in the long-term, over the next 5 to 10 years or more.

---

### Business Plan Questions

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IBusiness Plan Questions
    Who is the target audience? What challenge does the target audience face that the business can help solve?
    What is the fitness professional’s personal connection and motivation to help solve this problem?
    What does the business look like in 1, 5, and 10 years?

Executive Fitness Example1. Who is the target audience? What challenge does the target audience face that the business can help solve?

The target audience is busy business professionals who want to give their health, fitness, and nutrition a higher priority but have difficulty finding a sustainable path to achieve their goals.
2. What is the fitness professional’s personal connection and motivation to help solve this problem?

This business is owned by someone who, prior to becoming a fitness professional, worked a corporate office job. Firsthand experience with the lack of personal health goals in accomplished individuals is vast. Balancing a 40+-hour work week with family life, being sedentary, lacking motivation to exercise, and not understanding there is a sustainable path to health are all common realities. This allows for empathy with clients and for developing a plan that will fit their aspirations.
3. What does the business look like in 1, 5, and 10 years?

During the first year, systems will be developed and refined while the company services the professional market the owner is most familiar with—those working larger corporate jobs in upper to middle management. After the first year, using feedback from clients combined with additional market research, the company will be able to evolve and expand the service to other professionals in different markets, such as teachers and educators of all levels. The company will also start to bring on employees at that time to ensure high quality of service is maintained. At the 5-year mark, the business will have started partnering with corporations to provide in-house wellness services to employees, as well as continuing to grow the direct-to-client coaching services. At the 10-year mark, the business will be a large company in the online training industry, generating millions of dollars of revenue annually. This growth will require an entire professional management team, along with a strong corporate structure and a constantly updated and refined business plan.

---

### Company Description

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part ICompany DescriptionA description of the company should be concise yet detailed. Simply stating that a business will conduct online personal training is too vague. Conversely, going into detail about what estimated customer acquisition cost (CAC) will be is too in-depth for the executive summary (this will be included in expanded sections of the business plan such as marketing or financial). A description of the business should discuss the purpose of the business and what makes it unique. The uniqueness is often described as the unique selling proposition (USP). This is the place that the fitness professional should explain why customers will choose their business over a competitor.
Highlight the true uniqueness of the business. Mentioning features of a business are simply not enough. There was a time when responding to an e-mail within 24 hours or offering text/messaging to a client was unique and rare. However, now that there are more online coaching businesses, these features have become the standard. The fitness professional needs a USP that only they bring to their clients.
niroworld/Shutterstock.comIt is also a good idea, and common practice, to include information on any of the company founders, along with their backgrounds. Even if the business owner is a solopreneur, they should be able to sell themselves and explain why their background and experience makes them the most suited to bring their USP to the market.
The company description should also include the management structure of the company. If the business will be starting with partners or employees, their roles and responsibilities should be clearly defined here. Highlight why these individuals will be vital to the business’s initial success. If these employees are not included in the description and backgrounds of the company founders, then they should be included here.
Most entrepreneurs are solopreneurs at first. They are the only employee in the business. This means they are working both in and on the business. Take this opportunity to clarify what roles everyone involved will be taking on and what roles may be contracted out. For instance, most entrepreneurs will take on marketing, but few will do their own taxes.
Helpful HintThere are many gig services, companies that connect consumers or businesses with freelancers to perform various tasks or jobs, that can be found online for minimal cost. These can be utilized for things like creating infographics, editing blogs, and editing videos.

It is also possible to find reputable and affordable online services for things like accounting and legal advice.
Customer acquisition cost (CAC)How much a new customer costs a business – typically, the cost is determined through money spent on advertising.
Unique selling proposition (USP)The unique benefit or feature of a product or service that differentiates a business from other businesses offering a similar service or product.

---

### Business Plan Questions

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IBusiness Plan Questions
    What are the unique goals of the business?
    What makes the company unique to clients? What single thing does the business perform or understand better than anyone else in the industry?
    Who will the company consist of at inception? What roles will each person have?

Executive Fitness Example1. What are the short-term and long-term goals of the business?

The company’s short-term goals include developing systems to provide customized, but repeatable client experiences. This will help to achieve the long-term goal of increasing coaches and to provide sustainable, high quality, and personalized online fitness coaching to a growing number of clients with a well-trained staff.

2. What makes the company unique for clients? What single thing does the business perform or understand better than anyone else in the industry?

As business owners that had executive careers prior to transitioning to a career in fitness, any future trainers brought on board will also have that experience of having found the balance between work, family, and health. That is an experience that cannot be taught but must be learned to be appreciated. This will allow the trainers to empathize with and understand the client’s needs, wants, limitations, and goals better than anyone else in the industry.

3. Who will the company consist of at inception? What roles will each person have?

At inception, the company will consist of one owner-operator with goals to bring on up to four additional online trainers during a 2-year period. For the first year, the owner will balance their time between marketing, product development, sales, and servicing clients. At any time the owner feels overwhelmed in a specific area, help will be sought out in that specific area.

---

### Identify Target Audience

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IIdentify Target AudienceThe final section of the Executive Summary in the business plan should identify who the clients will be. Most business owners already have some idea of who their target audience is. Having too wide a target audience, the business’s message will get lost and not resonate with clients. A narrow target audience and will be too specific for anyone to hear the message. The target audience should be someone the fitness professional can relate to, that they know their USP will help, and that they feel will provide the business with enough clients to grow and expand.

Realize that every business will have clients who are outside of their target audience and their target audience will change over time, especially in the early stages of the business. For example, some fitness professionals may start out only wanting to work with competitive physique athletes, but quickly find out they prefer working with seniors who are trying to improve their quality of life.
Jirsack/Shutterstock.comBusiness Plan Questions
    What is the ideal client’s goal?
    Where does the ideal client live?
    How old is the ideal client?
    How long is the average client enrolled in coaching for?
    Are there any other specifics about the ideal client?

Executive Fitness Example1. What is the ideal client’s goal?

The ideal client’s goal is not just to lose weight or get into shape. The ideal client’s goal is to find a balance between life, work, and physical health.

2. Where does the ideal client live?

In order to provide prompt service to clients and properly manage the limited time a small business owner or business professional has, the ideal client is located in North America. Exact geographical location outside of that does not matter, as this is a virtual coaching company.

3. How old is the ideal client?

The ideal client is between the ages of 30 and 45 and is most likely settling into their second job in the same profession.

4. How long is the average client enrolled in coaching for?

Based on previous experience with in-person training and nutrition clients, the average client will need to be enrolled in the coaching program for at least 3 months before achieving their goals and possessing the knowledge to manage their own nutrition and training.

5. Are there any other specifics about the ideal client?

Based on both personal and professional experiences, the ideal client has one to two children under the age of 10. Experience in finding ways to balance training with family life, in addition to work, allows the company to bring a unique and sought-after service to clients.

---

### Products and Services

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IProducts and ServicesOnline Personal Training is not a product per se, but rather a service. The product description in the business plan must include detailed descriptions of what features will be brought to the market and what makes the product or service unique. Products and/or services should always be improving and changing. Offering the same service with no improvements is a quick way to become irrelevant in the highly competitive landscape of businesses, especially with online businesses. With that said, the downfall of many entrepreneurs is trying to perfect their product too much prior to actually having real-world feedback.
This section of the business plan should focus on the minimum viable product (MVP) that the business will be starting with. Remember the business plan is a living document that can be modified and updated as product offerings change. There are many approaches to developing an MVP. In this section, it will be broken down step-by-step to develop multiple product offerings that can get to market quickly and are easy to improve based on feedback from customers.
Helpful HintBefore starting to develop the MVP, do some simple market research and gain insight to what future competitors are doing. If the top competitors of the business are known, browse those companies’ websites, e-mail the companies with questions, and read their content. If the business’s competitors are unknown, search online for keywords that potential clients would most likely use to find services the business is going to offer. This is not to steal anything from competitors but, rather, to ensure the business is offering industry-standard items. The fitness professional may even find that what all competitor companies offer is not good enough for their clients and they will have the opportunity to build an industry-changing product.
Minimum viable product (MVP)A product that provides the bare minimum features to satisfy customer demand – this is the foundation for a product that will be developed and modified based on market feedback.

---

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IThe first step is to develop a base service. This base service forms the foundation for the USP and should be something the target market audience needs, as discussed in the previous section. Building on the example of offering online coaching to busy professionals, the base service will be set as Online Fitness Coaching.
Now that the product has been established, it is time to expand on what that product will actually include. These are the features and benefits. Benefits of a product will be discussed in the sales section of this chapter. For product development, the focus will be on features.
Features are the USP of a business’s services—essentially, what the service is. This is when the fitness professional will use their creativity and passion to accurately describe their product. To illustrate this point, here is an example of some simple features of a common dry-erase marker:

    Bright, vibrant colors
    Ergonomic fit to left or right hand
    Strong, durable casing
    Ability to write on multiple surfaces
    Easily erased
    Secure cap to prevent accidental markings
    Soft felt tip that does not require sharpening
    Disposable, no messy ink to refill

Mtsaride/Shutterstock.comThese features are not unique; they are features shared by almost all dry-erase markers. However, the fitness professional should be able to emphasize not only the uniqueness of their product, but uniquely emphasize the common features of their product. Now, using this strategy to find the features of Online Fitness Coaching, it is possible to develop answers for the example company.

Please note: This is not meant to be an all-inclusive list of what online coaching is or should be but simply a list for the specific example company that is being built.
Features and benefitsFeatures are descriptions of what a product or service offers customers – Benefits are how those features help solve a customer's problem.

---

### Features of Online Fitness Coaching Example

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IFeatures of Online Fitness Coaching Example
    Weekly check-ins with the coach that build toward sustainable fitness
    Custom training programs built around goals
    24/7 e-mail access to the coach with a guaranteed reply within 24 hours
    Access to the client-only newsletter, discussing the latest evidence-based fitness practices
    Coaching designed with flexibility in mind for individuals with busy schedules

This simple list of only five features highlights common offerings in online fitness coaching. However, most online coaches do not promote their common features (1 to 4) and simply highlight their USP (5). A common example of this in other industries is the Schlitz Beer company, which took their product to the top selling beer in the world simply by highlighting how beer was made as their USP. Their technique was standard in the beer industry, but no companies had mentioned how beer was made before Schlitz (Harter, 2018).
Business Plan Questions
    What are two common competitor features that the company might offer clients?
    What are two standard features that other companies are not highlighting?
    Describe the USP in 1 to 2 sentences.

The features have now been created for the product, Online Fitness Coaching. The example company officially has its first product. However, having one product is not good for the longer-term plan of gaining feedback and modifying the product. Therefore, a few offerings should be created based on the core product, Online Fitness Coaching.
Helpful HintFind balance when offering multiple products and services. Not only will the fitness professional put a lot of time and effort into developing products that may not be successful, it may actually hurt sales. Overchoice is when there are so many options in the marketplace that a buyer is overwhelmed and may choose to not purchase anything at all (Settle & Golden, 1974).

---

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IFirst, take the example company’s base product of Online Fitness Coaching and expanding it into three distinct product offerings:


    Online Exercise Coaching
    Online Nutrition Coaching
    Online Exercise and Nutrition Coaching (which contains both exercise and nutrition coaching)

The company will need to modify its USP or features from the original product to fit these expanded offerings. There will be some crossover and additional features. This is commonly seen in pricing charts, as seen in Table: Online Fitness Coaching Product Features Examples.
Online Fitness Coaching Product Features ExamplesOnline Exercise Coaching
Online Nutrition Coaching
Online Exercise and Nutrition Coaching
Weekly check-ins with the coach that build toward sustainable exercise habits
Weekly check-ins with the coach that build toward sustainable nutrition habits
Weekly check-ins with the coach that build toward sustainable exercise and nutrition habits
Custom exercise training programs built around goals and preferences
Custom nutrition program built around goals and preferences
Custom exercise and nutrition programs built around goals and preferences
24/7 email access to the coach with a guaranteed reply within 24 hours
24/7 email access to the coach with a guaranteed reply within 24 hours
24/7 email access to the coach with a guaranteed reply within 24 hours
Access to the client-only newsletter, discussing the latest evidence-based exercise practices
Access to the client-only newsletter, discussing the latest evidence-based nutrition practices
Access to the client only newsletter, discussing the latest evidence-based exercise and nutrition practices
Coaching designed with flexibility in mind for individuals with busy schedules
Coaching designed with flexibility in mind for individuals with busy schedules
Coaching designed with flexibility in mind for individuals with busy schedules
With this update, there are now three products to offer clients and test in the marketplace. Because of this, there have also been simple upgrades and add-ons developed for clients, which will increase sales. For instance, if a client enrolls in Online Exercise Coaching, but after a consultation with the business owner feels they should also partake in nutrition coaching, it is a simple upsell offer to Complete Online Fitness Coaching. This will also work in reverse to increase customer's lifetime value (CLV). If a client is canceling their services at $150 per month for Complete Online Fitness Coaching, the fitness professional can offer to downgrade the client to either nutrition or exercise coaching at only $100 per month.
Customer lifetime value (CLV)The amount that a customer will spend with business over a lifetime.

---

### Business Plan Questions

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IEach of these three offerings will be further developed to produce quality minimum viable products (MVPs) that will provide product add-ons, options, and a pricing structure that will be competitive in the marketplace.
Business Plan Questions
    What is the core service?
    What are two variations of that service and how do those variations relate to the core service?
    What is one unique feature for each of those new variations?

Executive Fitness Example1. What is the core service?

The core service for Executive Fitness is Complete Online Fitness Coaching, which includes both exercise and nutrition coaching.

2. What are two variations of that service and how do those variations relate to the core service?

Two components of Complete Online Fitness Coaching will be exercise and nutrition coaching. These can each be turned into a standalone service and then offered as the original, combined service. Both exercise and nutrition coaching are components of the core service that can be broken down into separate services to meet the demands and goals of clients.

3. What is one unique feature for each of those new variations?

Clients now have programs that are customized to focus on areas of their health that they feel they need the most support from a trainer on, instead of a one-size-fits-all package.

---

### Canned, Predesigned Programs

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part ICanned, Predesigned ProgramsMany online personal trainers seek to write predesigned exercise and nutrition programs and sell them as one-off purchases. This approach may not be the most sustainable path for all businesses to take. Just like working with an in-person training client, training programs should be customized for each person. A training program that works for a 28-year-old former college athlete who wants to focus on muscle hypertrophy most likely will not work for a 65-year-old who wants to improve their ability to carry grocery bags up the stairs.
Figure Customize Predesigned Training TemplatesConsider the customer lifetime value (CLV). A customer who makes a recurring monthly payment for customized training at $100 per month for 4 months is worth 16 times more than someone who pays $25 for a one-time exercise program. They are also more likely to refer clients back to the business versus the templated training program that can be e-mailed to a friend.

This is not to say templates do not have a place in business. Templates can be massive time savers for the busy fitness professional and can make processes and work more efficient and consistent. Having pre-made training templates that are easy for the business owner to modify and customize for each client will save hours of work developing programming for each client (Figure: Customize Predesigned Training Templates). While similar, this differs from using a generic approach to programming for every client. For instance, developing five training templates with no specific exercises can save time having to recreate the same thing for every client.
Here are some template examples:


    Template 1: A 2-day-per-week full-body program
    Template 2: A 3-day-per-week program broken down into push/pull/legs
    Template 3: A 5-day-per-week program consisting of back/biceps, shoulders/chest/triceps, and legs

Lastly, consider the customer acquisition cost (CAC). New customers are not free—they cost time, money, and energy. The business may not have a marketing budget, but the time that is spent making sales and creating content is time away from running the business and time away from providing services to clients. It is generally more cost effective to sell to someone who is already a customer than to try and acquire a new customer.

---

### Live One-on-One Coaching

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part ILive One-on-One CoachingNow that the three product offerings have been developed in nutrition, exercise/training, and nutrition and exercise coaching combined, it is time to build further on these products and provide some additional options to future clients. It is important to have valuable products for upsell to a client that they will benefit from. This will not only help clients increase their overall success but will also help boost the company's revenue as the CLV increases with each add-on. A simple option for this is to offer live video coaching for clients (Figure: Live Video Coaching). This is especially useful when the fitness professional is starting out and does not have a large client base. It enables the fitness professional to spend more time per client and obtain direct and instant feedback, and it provides more income per client for the business owner while they grow their business.
Continuing with the example company, three product offerings have been developed:


    Online Exercise Coaching
    Online Nutrition Coaching
    Complete Online Fitness Coaching

FigureLive Video CoachingA simple add-on for each of these products is a single-session video consultation. For a nutrition client, this could include a video call with the client while they go grocery shopping, being right there with them while they navigate the grocery store and work to make healthier food choices. For an exercise training client, this could include form checks or navigating the gym for a client who is nervous about going alone. Another option for add-ons is an in-person session. If the business owner has an online client who is local to their area, they could train with them at their gym, home, or outside (depending on their training/goals). For the nutrition client, they could actually go grocery shopping with them or help them learn how to meal prep. Adding these on to the product offerings, there is now a product line (Table: Upsell Options and Add-Ons).
Upsell Options and Add-OnsOnline Exercise Coaching
Online Nutrition Coaching
Online Exercise and Nutrition Coaching
Upsell option: Fitness and Nutrition Coaching
Upsell option: Fitness and Nutrition Coaching
Upsell option: N/A
Add-on option: Additional, 30-minute live video consult
Add-on option: Additional, 30-minute live video consult
Add-on option: Additional, 30-minute live video consult
With multiple coaching options, the chances have increased to upsell products to clients who will benefit from them, offer premium add-ons for existing clients, and keep the product overload to a minimum. The final step is pricing services and add-ons.

---

### Hybrid Model

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IHybrid ModelThe fitness professional also has the opportunity to build out their virtual coaching business with in-person clients, as well. This can allow the fitness professional to retain clients longer, take on a greater client load, and build their own online business while still working as an in-person trainer.
Options for this include the following:


    Working with nutrition clients online
    Intermediate- to advanced-level training clients who may not need direct supervision of a personal trainer but would like accountability and program design
    Utilizing client referrals for friends and family who may not be local to the training facility

Coach's CornerThe entrepreneur should keep their focus on their primary goal—their virtual coaching business. While the financial and employment stability that may come with an existing in-person training career is comfortable, the entrepreneur should ensure their attention is set on growing their online business. One of the many benefits of virtual coaching is the ability to take on more clients compared to in-person training. Spending valuable time with in-person training clients not only limits the personal trainer’s earning capability, but it can greatly slow down the rate of growth for the virtual business. The entrepreneur should find a balance between the two that steadily shifts focus toward the virtual coaching business.

---

### Market and Competitive Analysis

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IMarket and Competitive AnalysisFitness is an extremely competitive space, with almost 650,000 Americans employed by fitness or recreation centers and an expected growth rate of approximately 15% between 2018 to 2028 (Torpey, 2020; U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics, 2019). There is no difficulty in finding competitors in the fitness industry, no matter what niche a business targets. Analyzing the competitors in a market can be a daunting task; however, using a few key metrics (e.g., number of competitors in the market, average price of services, and the number of service variations offered) and incorporating a simplistic approach to designing pricing models and a target market, the fitness professional can save time and produce a quality business plan that will accelerate their growth by avoiding costly mistakes.
Rawpixel.com/Shutterstock.comWhen pricing any product, it is important to already know who the target market will be, what features will be offered with a well-defined product, and what variations of the product will be created. Once the fitness professional has these determined, they can take a look into the pricing of their competitors and accurately price themselves for the market. If the price is too low, there will not be enough revenue to pay the business owner a reasonable salary for work they have performed. If the price is too high, the business owner runs the risk of not having enough clients to pay themselves at all, or worse, they will have no clients. Many strategies exist for setting pricing for a new product. The most relevant and practical strategies will be broken down, including their benefits and downsides, and eventually one will be chosen for the example company. Before picking a pricing strategy, the fitness professional should conduct market research of future competitors in the area.
A simple online search for terms related to the target market and online coaching will produce ample results. Using the example company, a search for “online fitness coaching busy professionals” produced five quality results on the first page of the search engine. This provided pricing for five companies that offer online monthly coaching as follows in Table: Market Research Results Example.
Market Research Results ExampleCompany
1
2
3
4
5
Price
$199
$125
$595
$159
$139
The average price for monthly online coaching with these five companies is $243.

All of these companies offer similar services, each having their own unique USP. After this competitive analysis, it is known the average price in the market for online coaching is $243 per month and that the range in pricing for similar services is wide.

---

### Pricing Strategies

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IPricing StrategiesThe following are four different pricing strategies that will help guide the entrepreneur pricing strategy in a simple way:
Economy Pricing: Low priced, affordable pricing for everyone – Profit margins in this pricing category are small. This pricing strategy is designed to target a mass, broad market. In a service industry, this pricing strategy is typically reserved for large corporations.

Skimming Pricing: This pricing strategy charges a higher price at the start of the company and eventually lowers the pricing as more competitors enter the market. This strategy is typically used for companies entering a new market or market segment that is not very competitive.

Penetration Pricing: This strategy sets pricing lower at the start of the company in order to gain a share of the market quickly. Once the business has met its market share goals, prices are raised to a higher level. This strategy also allows for feedback from customers and helps with the decision on where a valid price point should be set.

Premium Pricing: Setting a high price is used to define the product or brand as a luxury item. In the mind of the consumer, the high price must mean a high-quality product. This strategy is used by high-end brands in the automotive and luxury watch industries.
Quality of Product and PricingQuality of Product
Price
Low
High
Low
Economy
Penetration
High
Skimming
Premium
Note that the entrepreneur may pivot at different points in time to different pricing strategies based on sales and feedback from customers. However, ensure that the business plan is updated to consider the new pricing strategy.

Whichever pricing model is chosen, price according to the target market. If the target market is college students, the pricing will need to be at the lower end of the market. If the target market is celebrities, then pricing will need to be at the higher end of the market.
Business Plan Questions
    Will the service best fit into an economy, penetration, skimming, or premium price strategy?
    Will the company charge a one-time, pre-paid package fee or a recurring monthly subscription?
    Will the company test other pricing strategies or price points?

Executive Fitness Example1. Will the service best fit into an economy, penetration, skimming, or premium price strategy?

The long-term goal of the service is to be priced as a premium service. To start the company and ensure that service quality commands a premium price point, services will be priced in the middle of the market, in a hybrid of penetration and premium pricing.

2. Will the company charge a one-time, pre-paid package fee or a recurring monthly subscription?

The product will be a recurring monthly subscription with a 3-month minimum subscription required. This will help to keep the cost lower, but still offer premium pricing. This will also allow the company to have a more predictable revenue stream.

3. Will the company test other pricing strategies or price points?

Utilizing feedback from clients and comparing that with projections and goals, the company will adjust pricing accordingly at 3-month intervals in the first year of business.

---

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IContinuing with the example company, because research showed there is a wide range of prices in the market, it is time to discover where the example company falls in the market. For this, a simple solution is to use a hybrid of the premium and penetration pricing models and be prepared to adjust based on feedback from clients. Setting a price around the average market price of $243 and deciding on $249 will put the business into a prime position for the average consumer. (Using an odd number for pricing is called psychological pricing—there is more resistance to $250 versus $249).
Helpful HintDo not change prices too often. Re-evaluate pricing every 3 months in the first year of business, every 6 months in the second year, and then yearly thereafter. Also, consider inflation in the economy. The average inflation rate is 3%, which means that prices will double about every 20 years. If prices are set at $100 for 10 years and that price is suddenly increased to $150 to offset inflation, many customers will be unhappy. However, a smart entrepreneur will plan for price increases. A good rule of thumb is a 5% price increase every other year or 10% every 3 years. This will help prevent a pricing shock to customers and a decline in purchasing power over time.
The example company now has a price of $249 for the coaching product, but still has three products. Pricing all three at the same rate would not give customers a price choice and makes it harder for the consumer to differentiate between products. $249 can be used for the base price or the upgrade price. The example company will use $249 as the upgrade and $199 for the base price, as a 25% upgrade price ($50 in this example) is a substantial revenue increase for the business, and a fair price for the client. Refer to Table: Base Price and Upgrade Price Example for the pricing options.
Base Price and Upgrade Price ExampleOnline Exercise Coaching
Online Nutrition Coaching
Complete Online Fitness Coaching
$199/month
$199/month
$249/month

### Content

Chapter 2Building a BusinessLesson 2Building a Virtual Coaching Business Plan: Part IThe company now has three products, each with their own pricing and the ability for clients to upgrade or downgrade. Downgrading is an important option to provide clients. It is better to have a client downgrade rather than leave altogether. Using these tools, the entrepreneur can create their own product lineup and pricing models that allow for products to be priced competitively in the market, upsell useful services to clients, and retain clients with different services that meet a client’s changing needs.
Helpful Hint
    Add-ons can either cost more than the original product and require less sales or can cost less but will require more sales.
    
        Cost more: less products that need to be created
        Cost less: will need new products more often to keep small amounts of money coming in
    
    



    After operating for 6 months, the entrepreneur should re-evaluate services that are selling and see if they can find where most of their revenue comes from.
    
        For example, if a business has 80% of their sales coming from 20% of their products (referred to as the Pareto Principle or the 80/20 principle), drop products that do not sell and/or do not generate enough revenue to warrant continued development.
    
    

Food for ThoughtTwo common pricing options include having many clients who pay a little or a few clients who pay a lot. Having a few clients who pay more typically means there will be fewer daily headaches. It is easier to communicate with 10 people versus 100. The tradeoff for that is if two clients leave, the business will lose 20% of its income. One of the important factors of pricing is to find a balance between workload and revenue security.

# Chapter 3: Technology Considerations

### Chapter 3: Technology Considerations

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsChapter 3: Technology Considerations Virtual training relies heavily on technology, which, when utilized properly, can improve training efficiency and outcomes, facilitate better communication and client relationships, and improve the ability to effectively manage more clients. From filming high-quality videos to choosing the correct video conferencing software, this chapter will review the necessary and ancillary technologies that will help fitness professionals develop, run, and scale a successful virtual training business.
Learning ObjectivesAfter completing this chapter, the NASM Virtual Coach will have the ability to do the following:


    Determine hardware, software, and operating system requirements needed to operate a virtual coaching business.
    Select an appropriate internet service and computer peripherals to suit the needs of a virtual coaching business.
    Identify fitness programming software to help manage and assess client progress.

---

### Introduction

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsIntroductionFitness professionals have an opportunity to use technology to develop, operate, and scale successful virtual training businesses. Clients are increasingly utilizing non-traditional, technologically driven fitness solutions, including mobile workout and food logging apps, wearable fitness technology, and virtual training programming. Accordingly, fitness professionals who are proficient in computer, mobile, and wearable technology can offer viable fitness solutions and meet the rapidly evolving needs of consumers within the digital market. This digital market expands the reach of a fitness professional from what was once just a local impact to now a global one.
Within the fitness industry, fitness professionals might find technology to be a major disruptor to the traditional approaches and methods in the delivery of services and client management. However, as technology continues to advance, fitness professionals have an opportunity to adopt new approaches to engage clients, deliver a quality service, and compete effectively in a demanding market. This chapter will explore the technological infrastructure, including hardware, software, peripherals, Internet service requirements, and wearable fitness technology considerations that are necessary to operate a successful virtual training business.
Viktoria Kazakova/Shutterstock.comTechnology Considerations Checklist A Technology Considerations Checklist is provided for the fitness professional to use throughout the chapter to track technology considerations, notate inventory the fitness professional may already own, and to suggest future technology purchases.
Technology Considerations Checklist

---

### Hardware Requirements

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsHardware Requirements Hardware includes the physical components that are required for a computer system to function. These physical components include a mouse or trackpad, keyboard, screen or monitor, and speakers. In addition, the physical components of processing hardware, which can be thought of like puzzle pieces (Figure: Physical Components of Processing Hardware), include the following:
FigurePhysical Components of Processing Hardware

---

### Computer Processing

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsComputer Processing The first puzzle piece of a computer’s hardware requirements is computer processing. When choosing a computer, it is important to ensure the processing capability aligns with the intended use of the machine. A personal home computer used for basic computing, some streaming, and Internet access will have significantly different processing needs than a computer intended for small business use. In order to make the best purchase for building a virtual coaching business, the fitness professional should understand what components and requirements are needed to operate effectively, efficiently, and productively.
The central processing unit, or CPU, is the brain of a computer (Figure: CPU Chip). It carries out and directs all tasks being performed. The faster a CPU is, the faster and more efficiently the computer will run. A fitness professional may be using Excel spreadsheets for exercise programming and have a video being uploaded to their workout directory for clients, while also using instant communication applications on their computer—all of which can consume processing power.
Weera.otp/Shutterstock.comFigureCPU ChipHaving enough processing capacity to keep a computer running at optimal pace, while multiple programs are running, can ensure productive workflow.
CPU speed is currently measured in gigahertz (GHz), but, historically, when processing power was lower, they were in megahertz (MHz). A gigahertz is also known as “Clock Speed.” At 2.5 GHz, it is like a clock that ticks 2.5 billion times in a second. Each tick is an opportunity for the CPU to move and modify the smallest fragments of data called bits. The higher the GHz classification for a processor, the quicker a processor can process data and make decisions. Modern computers typically range from 2.0 GHz through to 5 GHz or more as technology advances. While top-of-the-line processor speeds are not needed for virtual coaching, the fitness professional should ensure the machine being purchased has between 2.4 and 4.0 GHz of processing speed.
Getting TechnicalAnother component of CPU technology to consider is how many cores the processor has. Each core can work on a single task. The more cores a processor has, the more tasks a CPU can process. An average computer may come with a dual core processor but can contain more. Ideally, aiming to purchase a machine with four cores or more will help with multitasking and running multiple programs without slowing the computer down.
Central processing unit (CPU)A CPU is the brain of a computer. It carries out and directs all tasks being performed.
GigahertzThe measurement of how fast a computer processor can manage bits of data – a 1.0 GHz processor can process 1 million bits of data per second; a 2.4 GHz processor can process 2.4 million bits of data per second.
BitsThe smallest fragments of computer data.

---

### RAM

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsRAMRAM stands for Random Access Memory and is another piece of the puzzle that makes up a computer system (Figure: RAM). RAM is essentially a computer's short-term memory.
daniiD/Shutterstock.comFigureRAMRAM temporarily stores the information that the computer needs to access immediately or in the near future.

RAM keeps track of everything that is being processed by the computer such as open web browser tabs, a training spreadsheet being edited, or a video being rendered. Instead of the computer going into a hard drive storage device to pull the data it requires, RAM knows what the computer will need next and keeps that information at hand, ready to be used.
For example, RAM is like having a grocery shopping list in hand at the store. Having this list shortens the shopping trip; without it, the shopper would walk aimlessly up and down the aisle trying to remember what is needed for groceries and extend the time spent shopping!
Getting TechnicalRAM is short-term memory, whereas a hard drive is used for long-term storage. In fact, RAM does not actually store anything. RAM is what is considered volatile memory, meaning it loses its data as soon as the power is shut off. The data and information are only housed temporarily, as needed. Hard drives are considered non-volatile memory, meaning the data is stored even when the power is turned off.
RAMRandom Access Memory – A computer's short-term memory for processing immediate and upcoming tasks.

---

### How Much RAM is Needed?

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsHow Much RAM is Needed? RAM is currently measured in Gigabytes (GB). How many GB of RAM a computer has will determine how much data it can temporarily store for instant access as the computer is operating. The amount of RAM needed will depend mainly on what the computer is being used for and the complexity of the program. The more complicated a program becomes the more RAM will be required. A simple program, such as a basic notepad, will not take up much RAM, if any. Complex programs like Excel, Photoshop, or a video-editing software may use gigabytes of RAM by themselves.
With virtual coaching requiring programs like Excel spreadsheets, video editing software, or video conferencing programs that can consume a large amount of RAM by themselves, it is a good idea to ensure you have enough RAM rather than not enough (Table: RAM Recommendations). This can also minimize expensive RAM add-on kits or having to purchase a new computer if there is no option to expand RAM capabilities. It saves money in the long-term and minimizes frustrations with a slow operating computer system.
RAM Recommendations<4 GB
Minimum requirements for operating systems and basic function.
4 GB to 8 GB
Minimum requirements for typical computer use.
8 GB to 16 GB
Provides enough RAM to support most or all tasks except high-end video editing or game rendering.
16 GB or more
Professional-grade editing software, gaming, and content rendering.
Coach’s CornerIn most cases, 8 to 16 GB of RAM will be enough to effectively and efficiently run a virtual coaching business. When purchasing a laptop or desktop, ensuring it has a minimum of 8 GB of RAM, is a great starting point. 16 GB of RAM would be enough for the majority of the process and software requirements for running a virtual coaching business.
Food for ThoughtMore RAM is not always better. While it is a good idea to get more than needed, rather than not enough, getting 32 GB of RAM when the max operating requirements are less than 16 GB may be wasteful. It costs more money and does not make the computer faster unless the software programs actually need 16 GB+ of RAM.

---

### Storage

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsStorageA third piece of the computer hardware requirements puzzle is storage. With virtual coaching, the fitness professional is responsible for not only handling and organizing client information, but also managing and storing business documents such as a business plan, workout demonstration videos, and any of created social media content. From liability and sign up forms, to progress photos, workout plans, and private client information, being able to safely store this data as well as quickly access it as needed is key to running a successful virtual coaching business. There are two main options for data storage (Table: Types of Storage):


    Local Data Storage
    Cloud or Online Storage

Types of StorageLocal Data Storage
Cloud or Online Storage
What it is
Hard copy of the information or data

Examples include the following:


    USB flash drives
    Internal hard drives
    External hard drives

Information stored on the Internet, on a server

Examples include the following:


    Google Drive
    Dropbox
    iCloud

Accessibility

    Harder to access on the go, must carry a physical hard drive


    Easy to access on the go from smartphone or laptop

Security

    Increased risk of physical drives being stolen, lost, or damaged
    Added security because the fitness professional controls who has access to storage devices


    No risk of data loss due to mishandling, damage, or loss of a device
    Increased risk of data-hacking or online data theft due to third-party servers hosting data

Scalability

    Expensive to scale due to needing to use physical hard drives
    Physical hard drives take up more physical space


    Added cloud storage costs minimal compared to physical hard drives

Speed of Use

    Faster than downloading or uploading files from cloud-based storage


    Large files can be time consuming to upload or download

---

### Local Storage

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsLocal StorageLocal storage has the benefit of instant access and is typically faster than uploading and downloading information from the cloud. Plus, if an Internet connection goes offline temporarily, a virtual coaching business can still operate because of the data being accessible on demand, as needed. Local storage devices such as thumb drives, the physical storage device on the computer, or an external drive (Figure: Local Storage) are also more secure than cloud storage because information can only be physically accessed with those storage devices in hand. However, if those drives become lost, stolen, or crash, there is a risk of data loss.
There are two main types of hard drives to consider for local storage:


    Hard Disk Drive or HDD: A physical disk that spins inside a case where the data is written and erased. They are typically cheaper but also larger and slower than other types of hard drives.
    Solid-State Drive or SSD: A solid-state storage device is similar to a thumb drive. It has no moving parts and is faster than a standard HDD but also comes at a higher cost.

Anton Marchenkov/Shutterstock.comFigureLocal StorageFood for ThoughtConsider getting an external hard drive that is kept at home, which regularly backs up all the vital information for running a virtual coaching business. If a computer or phone is lost, or something gets damaged, that external drive will safely store all that data and it is easily accessible.
Hard disk drive (HDD)A data-storage device, which has a physical spinning disk where information or data is stored, written, or deleted.
Solid-state drive (SSD)A data-storage device that uses no physical spinning or moving parts to store, write, or delete data.

---

### Cloud Storage

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsCloud StorageChristian Horz/Shutterstock.comCloud storage has the advantage of being accessed on the go with most cloud services being accessible from a phone or laptop. This means a virtual coaching business can be easily conducted from a smartphone or laptop and a Wi-Fi signal. However, there may be higher security risks with cloud-based storage. Because the data is stored in a third-party system, there may be some added risk of attackers hacking into the system and gaining access to the data (Tujilio et al., 2016). Alongside added security risks, large file uploads and downloads may be more time consuming than transferring between a computer and a physical external hard drive.

When picking a cloud storage service, be mindful of scaling a virtual coaching business. Dropbox and Google Drive both come with word processing and spreadsheet capabilities and a large amount of initial free storage. Google Drive has some added benefits such as integrated e-mail and business services and other professional software such as Google Sheets (spreadsheets), Google Docs (word processing software), and the ability to create forms for collecting client information and sign ups.

Both local storage and cloud storage have their pros and cons, and using a mixed approach of both cloud storage and local storage will be most effective for managing and running a virtual coaching business.

---

### Video Card

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsVideo CardThe final piece, a video card, or graphics card, is hardware that takes all the data from across a computer system and generates it into a visible image on the computer monitor or laptop screen (Figure: Video Card). Most video cards include a stand-alone processing unit, built-in memory that is similar to RAM, and sometimes a cooling fan to help manage temperatures. It is essentially another computer in itself. Just like RAM, a video card is designed to take added stress off the CPU to more efficiently process images and transfer them to the monitor or screen.
teh_z1b/Shutterstock.comFigureVideo CardMany graphics cards will also have additional outputs such as an HDMI connection or VGA connection. HDMI connections transmit both audio and video data, whereas a VGA connection only transmits video data. Some graphics cards will also include an S-video display and USB port, which can connect to digital cameras, webcams, and other peripherals like microphones.

For doing high-quality exercise videos, filming and editing social media content, or creating PowerPoint presentations, the built-in video card that comes with any mid-range desktop or laptop will meet the needs of a virtual coaching business. Upgrading video cards might be considered if the machine being purchased will be used for high-definition video editing, gaming, or other professional editing software uses.
Video cardA video card or graphics card is a piece of hardware that takes all the data from across a computer system and generates it into a visible image on the computer monitor or laptop screen.

---

### Desktop or Laptop?

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 1Hardware RequirementsDesktop or Laptop? A fitness professional should consider whether a desktop computer or laptop computer will be needed to build a virtual coaching business. There is more available processing power in a typical desktop computer than in a laptop computer when looking at a comparable price range. While a laptop has all of the hardware requirements built into it as a complete package, a desktop computer will have an added cost of the wires, monitor, speakers, mouse, and keyboard. Once these pieces are factored in, a high-quality laptop can be purchased for a similar price with more than enough processing capabilities for a virtual coaching business.

If building a virtual coaching business with a home base in mind and mobility is not a primary consideration, a desktop computer will give the most capability to the end user. However, the mobility of a laptop often outweighs the benefits of a desktop computer when it comes to running a virtual coaching business. A laptop can be used in a variety of spaces such as a coffee shop, throughout a home, and when travelling. All that is needed is a stable Internet connection.
Africa Studio/Shuttershock.com

---

### Software Requirements

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsSoftware Requirements Software components are the actual programs running on a computer system to make it function and operate. Software is strings of programming code that are run and processed by the hardware components of the computer system. For example, software is found in the form of operating systems themselves such as Windows Operating System from Microsoft or iOS from Apple, to programs such as the Word processor, Excel, video games, and even just music players. This topic will cover operating systems, video conferencing systems, and other software recommendations to help the fitness professional operate in the virtual coaching space effectively and efficiently.
Operating Software There are currently three major operating systems dominating the market in computer technology. Microsoft with their Windows Operating System, Apple with their iOS and OS-X operating systems, and Linux, which is used as a baseline in many systems like Roku TVs, social media feeds, and smart technology integrated into the automotive industry. In 2019, Microsoft dominated the software industry with a worldwide market share of 77% with Windows and Apple Inc. with OS-X around 17% (Statcounter GlobalStats, 2020). Both Microsoft and Apple operating systems have their own unique pros and cons.

The primary consideration for purchasing a computer system and for which operating system to use will always be governed by the fitness professional’s budget. Apple products have always carried a higher price tag when compared to similar performing Windows-based computers.
Aa Amie/Shutterstock.comFood for ThoughtThe 2019 baseline MacBook Air was priced at $1,199 USD in 2019. A similarly performing Windows-based laptop was priced around $200 cheaper. Higher-performing Apple computers can be even more expensive, ranging upward of $2,500 dollars.

---

### Content

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsApple operating systems do come with some extra software and programming that may need to be purchased on a Windows-based operating system. With iMovie, which can be used to edit videos, merge sound clips, and add subtitles, for example, purchasing third-party video editing software may not be needed. However, Windows third-party software compatibility shadows the Apple operating system.
The notable difference between the Windows and Apple operating systems will be in the user experience and interface. For example, the OS-X system from Apple uses a pop-up app dock for favorite apps, whereas Windows OS has a taskbar and a start menu. Windows has a more versatile file management system, whereas OS-X offers a higher degree of integration with other Apple products. While the brands themselves and loyal fans may proclaim that one operating system is better, preference becomes the main determining factor of how to choose which OS to use.
Overall, the main determining factors when choosing an operating system to fit the needs of a virtual coaching business should be the following:


    Budget
    Intended use, such as added video editing
    Ease of use, with peripherals like tablets or wearable fitness trackers
    Preference, user experience, and interface
    Hardware

Critical!The fitness professional does not need to purchase a new computer or components. Purchasing a preowned computer can save money when on a tighter budget.

---

### Video Conferencing Software

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsVideo Conferencing Software FigureVisual ConnectionsVideo conferencing technology is a critical channel of communication for virtual training. Not only does video conferencing eliminate the barriers of geographical location, it allows fitness professionals to engage and connect with clients in a more meaningful manner.

In a study on processing information, humans process visual information more quickly and capably than through audible or written information (Cohen et al., 2009). This transfers to improved engagement and also client understanding, which are crucial components of successful client outcomes. Regular eye contact throughout video calls may result in higher perceived friendliness and improved information recall. A pleasant, smiling facial expression can indicate openness and positively strengthen the interaction between the fitness professional and the client (Park & Park, 2018) (Figure: Visual Connections).

---

### Content

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsIn addition, studies have shown that body language and tone of voice contribute to 55% and 38%, respectively, of effective communication; while words, such as a phone call or written message, only account for around 7% (Park & Park, 2018). Video conferencing allows the fitness professional, and the client, to convey emotion and tonality in a conversation or virtual training session that may otherwise get lost through other forms of communication. Some examples might include the following:


    The fitness professional can sense if the client has encountered some obstacles if the tone of the conversation shifts.
    The fitness professional can share in a client’s excitement if they hit a personal record in one of their exercises.
    The fitness professional can explain and demonstrate proper exercise form.
    When having difficult conversations, the client can hear the fitness professional and see if they are paying full attention, helping the client find solutions.

There is no shortage of options when it comes to video conferencing. When choosing a video conferencing software, the user should consider factors such as price, participant and meeting time thresholds, mobile app accompaniment, and other features such as meeting recording. While many video conferencing applications are available for free, it is not uncommon for them to have restrictions on the number of attendees and meeting runtimes. Several software vendors offer the user the ability to upgrade to a paid version, which removes the aforementioned restrictions. The following table outlines some of the more popular options currently available; however, these metrics are subject to change (Table: Comparing Video Conferencing Software). Before choosing a video conferencing service, investigate if the company has changed their terms and conditions.
Comparing Video Conferencing SoftwareSoftware
Paid/Free
Maximum No. of Participants
Maximum Meeting Runtime

### Word Processing Software

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsWord Processing SoftwareWord processing software is another common communication tool used in virtual coaching. Word processing software is essential to the development of coaching plans and other personalized content intended for sharing with a client. It can also be used for record keeping and business administration activities such as scheduling and invoicing. Most computers are equipped with a trial version word processing software as a standard feature.
The standard software will vary depending on the operating system. For example, the Windows operating system is commonly equipped with Microsoft Word, whereas the Apple OS-X operating system is commonly equipped with Pages. Most trial version word processing software is restrictive in the features necessary to create, edit, save, and share content, which means the fitness professional may need to purchase additional software.
In addition to standard equipped software or purchased software, a user may choose to use a cloud-based word processing program. While standard equipped word processing software does not vary remarkably between programs, there are considerable differences between a cloud-based and non-cloud-based word processing software, specifically in the ability to collaborate in real time.
When choosing a word processing software, the user should consider cost, compatibility with the operating system, and features. Cloud-based versions of commonly recognized software, such as Microsoft Word or Google Docs, are often seen as advantageous due to the collaboration functionality. This allows multiple users to view and edit documents in real time with 24/7 access by both client and coach and saved version history. This functionality could be viewed as beneficial and more efficient in providing client feedback than a non-cloud-based software.

---

### Spreadsheets

Chapter 3Technology Considerations Lesson 2Software RequirementsSpreadsheetsThe fitness professional would benefit from spreadsheet software. This software can allow the fitness professional to create workout programs, invoice clients, or build client metric monitoring sheets, among many other uses (Figure: Example Spreadsheet). Google Sheets, Numbers from Apple Inc., or Excel from Microsoft are all examples of spreadsheet programs.

This program, alongside a word processing application, may be all a fitness professional needs to operate their business. One consideration, however, if business begins to scale, is that there are apps and coaching software programs that can improve time management and may be easier for the client to use.
PixieMe/Shuttershock.comFigureExample Spreadsheet
