### Customizing the Sales Process

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 2Sales Goals and Process CustomizationCustomizing the Sales Process With an automated prospecting process, the fitness professional can provide a highly customized sales process designed to appeal to different subsets of their target market. The process of subdividing target markets is called market segmentation. Segmenting a marketing or sales list will allow the fitness professional to deliver highly customized and personalized sales messaging directly to leads.
Email list segmentation can be implemented starting at the prospect level. Specific marketing content can be created for different segments of the target audience. For example, a blog post about resistance training for busy mothers will attract a different segment of the same target audience when compared to a blog post about endurance training for busy mothers. The target audience is the same, but the segments, those interested in resistance training versus those interested in endurance training, are different.

Segmenting the target audience can be automated for the sales process when the prospect becomes a lead by providing their email address in exchange for an offer. The lead is then segmented into an appropriate email marketing list that contains the same basic information as other email workflows but will have more personalized sales copy that conveys an understanding and solution to the segment's problems.
Red Deer/Shutterstock.com

---

### Educating Prospective Clients

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 2Sales Goals and Process CustomizationEducating Prospective ClientsThe purpose of the marketing strategy is to educate prospective clients about the existence of the company and its programs and services. The sales process continues educating leads about the solutions the fitness professional provides to the problems they are facing. This education should be customized to a lead’s segment as much as possible. Every lead will prefer to be educated on the aspects of the programs or services that directly apply to their individual needs. Two education techniques include offering a unique selling proposition and value proposition.
Offering a Unique Selling PropositionA unique selling proposition (USP) is a specific benefit a product or service provides the target market that separates a business from its competitors. It is important to highlight the true uniqueness of the programs and services offered. Mentioning features of a business or its services is simply not enough. There was a time when responding to an e-mail within 24 hours or offering text messaging to a client was unique and rare. However, at a time when more online coaching businesses are appearing, these features have now become the standard. The fitness business entrepreneur needs a USP that only they are able to bring to their clients. While the USP can be the same for different lead segments, it should be positioned differently to address the unique concerns of a particular segment.
Providing ValueThe value proposition is simply the value of the programs and services that are offered. This can describe the price of the service, service quality, and value trade-off, which is the lead saving money by choosing the service offered instead of spending more money on another option. Value propositions should educate leads on the value of the programs or services and be utilized to support the USP.

---

### Customer Onboarding Process

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessCustomer Onboarding Process Developing a systematic process around the customer sales cycle or sales strategy allows for tracking, refinement, directional pivots, reproducibility/scale, and efficiency in client acquisition. Prior to becoming paid clients, consumers will enter different stages based on where they are in the sales process. As mentioned previously, these sales stages are prospects, leads, and clients.
Prospects are potential customers who are just finding out about the service. This could be by reading a blog, watching a video, or simply browsing the fitness professional’s website. Prospects are starting to show interest in the business’s services; however, they have shown no interest beyond that.
Leads are prospects who have shown a greater-than-normal interest in the business’s product or service. Typically, a lead has reached out to the fitness professional in some form and has provided some type of personal information to initiate interaction and conversation. This contact information can come from filling out a form on the fitness professional’s website, entering an e-mail address in exchange for a free ebook, or interacting with the fitness professional on social media.
Customers or clients are leads who have become paid customers of the business. Once a person becomes a client, they do not simply stay at this stage. Existing clients can also re-enter a sales funnel and become a lead for service or product upgrades and add-ons. Clients need to be nurtured as much, if not more, than prospects and leads to ensure their retention.

Please note: Both prospects and leads can become clients—they do not have to follow the specific order or progression outlined to become a client. The prospect, lead, and customer path is simply the most common progression from consumer to paid customer.
ProspectA person whom the business has identified as fitting their client criteria but has not yet provided their information.
LeadsProspects who have shown interest in a company and have provided identifying information, such as an email address.
CustomersLeads who have purchased a product or service from a company; may also be used interchangeably with the term clients.

---

### Customer Journey

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessCustomer Journey The fitness business entrepreneur should develop an understanding of what their client’s customer journey will be as they progress from prospect to client. This journey or process can also be referred to as the sales funnel. Mapping the customer journey will allow the fitness professional to develop a repeatable process that can be modified based on real-world results and feedback. The customer journey will tie in closely with marketing efforts. The sales aspect of the customer journey will essentially be an expanded and more detailed outline derived from the fitness professional’s marketing strategy.
An important aspect of the customer journey process is dividing potential customers into different subcategories. Separating prospects into groups based on certain predetermined characteristics is called market segmentation. Market segmentation allows the fitness professional to build more effective sales strategies based on consumer characteristics and where a prospect is in their customer journey. A potential client who visits one blog on a company's website needs a different marketing strategy than a potential client who follows a call to action and provides their e-mail address in exchange for a free ebook.
Using a fictitious company, here is an example of a possible customer journey map (Figure: Customer Journey Map):
FigureCustomer Journey Map
    A consumer reads a blog article the fitness professional posted and becomes a prospect. 
    
        How the prospect found the blog article is detailed in the marketing strategy and may include paid search engine advertising, social media sharing, and organic search results.
        Other content examples that may be used to drive prospect engagement include blogs, videos, infographics, social media posts, and podcasts.
    
    
    The prospect provides their contact information to the business and is turned into a lead. 
    
        The incentive for the prospect to provide this information is a detail from the marketing strategy by following a call to action.
        Content that can be used to turn a prospect into a lead include free digital content offers (training template, webinars, ebooks, etc.), filling out a contact form, or interacting with the business on social media.
    
    
    The lead is nurtured and turned into a client. 
    
        Lead nurturing is a tie-in between marketing and sales. Details about how leads will be nurtured are provided from the marketing strategy. No matter the lead nurturing strategy, the sales goal is the same.
        Examples of lead nurturing include automated e-mail marketing campaigns, phone calls, and text messaging.
    
    

Customer journey processThe guided path, designed by the business, that a consumer will follow to turn into a customer.
Market segmentationDividing prospects and leads to more well-defined categories.
Call to actionA prompt from a business for a consumer to take an action.

---

### Sales Plan Questions for Customer Journey Process

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessWhile this is a basic example of the journey from prospect to client, not all prospects will become leads and not all leads will become clients. If a prospect does not provide a business with contact information, that does not automatically mean they cannot become a lead at some point in the future. A lead can be subdivided into two categories: warm and hot lead. A warm lead does not provide the business with contact information. However, the business can use paid advertising to initiate a retargeting campaign and subsequent sales funnel.
A hot lead is a client who has provided the business with their contact information in exchange for an offer from the company, as mentioned in previous examples. When a hot lead drops off and stops responding, they can be placed into yet another sales funnel that perhaps offers a discount on the program or service or even a free trial to further incentivize the lead into accepting a call to action. In this situation, the additional offer helps to continue building trust and rapport with the lead.
Sales Plan Questions for Customer Journey Process
    What are three types of content that can be used to attract prospects? How can the content be created and managed?
    What are two types of free, high-quality content that can be used to turn prospects into leads? What offer will be presented to the prospect in exchange for their contact information.
    What are two methods in which leads will be nurtured into clients? What is your lead nurturing process?

Warm leadA lead that is interested but has not provided the business with contact information.
Retargeting campaignA marketing campaign that targets specific segments of prospects or leads that have not yet become customers.
Hot leadA lead that is interested and has provided the business with contact information.

---

### From Client to Brand Champion

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessFrom Client to Brand ChampionFor many businesses, once a prospect becomes a client, the sales process is over. The customer journey process has achieved its goal and the business has a new client. However, for the fitness professional entrepreneur, the process has only just begun. Because it is easier and more cost-effective to sell to existing customers compared to acquiring new customers, the fitness professional should focus on maximizing a  customer's lifetime value (CLV). This can be done through delivering exceptional service, product add-ons, and upgrades. Upgrading a client who enrolled in online fitness training to online training plus nutrition coaching will result in a more profitable customer while also providing the customer with more tools to achieve their goals and overall success.
Food for ThoughtImagine that a client pays $200 per month for virtual fitness coaching. Assuming that the client has an average membership length of 4 months, the lifetime value of this client is $800. If this client upgrades to a package that includes virtual fitness and nutrition coaching at $250 per month, after their first month, the client has a lifetime value of $950. This is a 16% increase in revenue from the client’s original lifetime value. Over the course of a year, if 100 clients purchase the same upgrade, this would result in an extra $15,000 in revenue for the business.
Upgrading an existing client's services is yet another customer journey process. The previous customer journey examined turning a prospect into a client; however, turning an existing client into a brand champion client requires a different process. A champion is a client who has a higher than average CLV. The brand champion client is a champion of your business and your brand. They are a long-term client who purchases service upgrades and raves about the business to everyone they know. Not every client will become a brand champion, but the virtual fitness entrepreneur must have processes in place to be able to take clients to the champion level.
Customer lifetime value (CLV)The amount that a customer will spend with business over a lifetime.

---

### Content

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessA referral program can be a great way to turn clients into champions. A referral program is a relatively easy sales process to automate. A referral program can be as simple as sending an automated e-mail to all clients every 30 days informing them that for every three clients they refer to the business, they will receive 1 month of virtual coaching free. This simple method can turn successful clients into salespeople for the business. For example, if the business has 100 clients at an average CLV of $800 each, obtaining three referrals from just 5% of existing clients would potentially result in an $11,000 per year profit, simply by sending an e-mail.

The final customer journey that will be illustrated is that of the former client. Every business should target former clients. Former clients are already familiar with the business and can be considered a warm lead because they have already provided their contact information and paid for programs or services previously. Again, basic e-mail automation can simplify this process for the already busy virtual fitness professional. Re-engaging former clients can be as simple as sending them an e-mail every 60 days to check-in, see how they are doing, and offer a 10% discount if they return to virtual training or coaching. As an example, if just 5% of a list of 500 former clients re-enroll in coaching per year, assuming an average CLV of $800 ($200 per month x 4 months), the business will have increased its annual revenue by $20,000 simply by sending an e-mail.
elenabsl/Shutterstock.comThe customer journey is not just one process but many depending on where the customer, client, or former client are currently in their lifetime cycle with the fitness professional. The fitness professional entrepreneur who establishes processes that cover every aspect possible of a customer’s journey has set up their business for steady growth and long-term success.

---

### Sales Process

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessSales ProcessThe sales process is what happens after a prospect becomes a lead. The sales process is the journey that leads take to become paying clients. During the previous phase of prospecting for new clients, the consumer turned into a lead. At that point, the marketing process ended, and the sale process began.
The lead-to-client sales process is one that will illustrate to qualified leads that the fitness professional understands their needs, help build rapport, and show the potential client their concerns and desires are being heard. This allows the fitness professional to demonstrate their expertise in the area the lead is struggling with. This process is commonly referred to as lead nurturing. In an automated sales process, lead nurturing can easily be done via e-mail marketing.
With e-mail marketing automation, the fitness professional has the equivalent of highly personalized ads sent directly to their target audience who has already shown specific interest in their products or services. E-mail automation provides the fitness professional with an opportunity to perform market segmentation and provide highly personalized e-mails. E-mail automation should always be as segmented as possible to ensure the most personalized messaging possible is being delivered to the lead.
Eny Setiyowati/Shutterstock.comNot everyone on a fitness professional’s e-mail marketing list is a lead. When someone is first added to an e-mail list, they are a lead. This means the marketing strategy used by the fitness professional worked and a prospect was turned into a lead. The prospect showed interest, accepted an offer, and opted-in to an e-mail list and became a lead. The lead is then placed into e-mail automation software and enters into a specific e-mail automation workflow for new leads. This workflow is designed to provide e-mails that will build rapport and increase trust with leads. This is typically done over several days or weeks. By the time the workflow is complete, one of three things will happen:

    The lead becomes a client. The workflow was successful. Once the lead becomes a client, the new lead workflow ends immediately.
    The lead unsubscribes from the workflow. This can happen for any number of reasons. Please note: If the unsubscribe rate is more than .3% on the initial e-mail, then the content of the e-mails in the workflow should be assessed/re-examined for relevancy. Any of the following can influence open and unsubscribe rates and should be examined: the subject line copy, e-mail body copy, frequency or quantity of e-mails, day and/or time sent, and content design reassessed.
    The workflow ends and the lead does not become a client. The sales process did not work and the lead did not initiate a purchase.

In the outcome where the lead did not become a client, they are no longer a lead but are once again a prospect. They will still receive general marketing e-mails as part of the marketing strategy; however, they would no longer receive personalized lead nurturing e-mails. At this point, the prospect will need to be remarketed to until they are placed into another sales funnel or workflow.
Sales processThe journey of turning a lead into a client.
Lead nurturingThe process of educating, displaying empathy, understanding, and listening to a lead to turn the lead into a customer.
WorkflowA systematic and repeatable process.

---

### Sales Process Example

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessSales Process ExampleImagine a prospect has entered the sales process after providing their e-mail address on the company's website in exchange for a free ebook. Instantly, the newly created lead receives this email in their inbox.
Email 1
Subject: No Frustration Home Workouts

Body:

Hi, Joe,

Many people think that working out at home requires expensive equipment, a lot of room, and time that they just don’t have. You’re about to find out that none of that is true. As requested, this email contains your free access to an at-home fitness guide that will show you how to design easy home workouts without expensive equipment in any sized space and in time increments that you can manage.

Download Self-Guided Home Workouts here.

Please reach out to me at any time with any questions that you have.

John Smith, NASM-CPT

Virtual Fitness Coach

P.S. I will send you an email tomorrow covering the most common questions that I receive about the ebook.


 
The email containing the ebook download was sent immediately to the new lead, helping build trust by delivering on the promise made. The email addresses the lead by name, reminds the lead about how the offer (ebook download) can help solve a problem, and then delivers the solution by providing the ebook. At the bottom of the email are the next steps, which indicate they will receive an email the following day that contains answers to some of the most common questions regarding the ebook topics.
This initial e-mail accomplishes the following goals:


    Builds trust
    Shows an understanding of the problem the client wants to solve
    Provides a solution and evidence of expertise in the problem area (e-book)
    Outlines expectations for next steps
    Demonstrates a genuine interest in the lead with the follow-up Q & A email

Exactly 24 hours later, the next email is sent.

---

### Email 2

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessEmail 2
Subject: Answers to Your Questions, as Promised

Body:

Hi, Joe,

"Working out is difficult and time consuming.” This is the most common issue that I hear as a virtual fitness coach. Many people don’t feel like they have the time or skill to work out effectively from home. However, the truth is that anyone can work out effectively from home and achieve the results they want. As I mentioned in yesterday’s e-mail, here are the most common questions I receive:

Q: What if I’m not strong enough to do some of the exercises like a pushup?

A: Eventually, you will be. With consistency, patience, and practice, you’ll be able to do a push-up in no time. Until you can, you can modify any exercise so that you’re able to perform it (more on how to modify exercises shortly).

Q: How long should these home workouts take?

A: There is no set amount of time to work out. If you’re able to work out for 45 minutes at once, perfect. If you’re only able to work out in two 5-minute intervals per day, then that is perfect too.

Q: How long will it take for me to see results?

A: That will vary by person. However, there are ways to get noticeable results much faster than others. In fact, that’s why I offer online fitness coaching with completely custom designed exercise and nutrition coaching to get you the results that you want and need. If you’re interested in learning more about teaming up with me for online coaching, click this link to schedule a free 20-minute phone consultation.

If the ebook yesterday made you feel a little overwhelmed with designing your own home workout, that’s fine! Here is an example of a home workout that I designed for a client. This workout lists the modifications for exercises in case you have some difficulty with them at first. Give this workout a try tomorrow and I will check-in in 2 days to see how it went.

John Smith, NASM-CPT

Virtual Fitness Coach

P.S. I’ll also be sending you my free nutrition guide in the next email. So, make sure you keep an eye out for the email!


 
The second email was delivered exactly when promised, as indicated in the subject line. It begins with an objection that is broad, but one that people genuinely have about fitness training. The email then proceeds to present the lead with an additional offer to accept/opt-into a free workout to try at home. Finally, the email ends again with a clearly defined next step. The fitness professional will check-in two days after the lead completed the workout. The email also incentivizes the lead to open the follow-up email because there is another free guide coming, only this time focused on nutrition, as well as a surprise bonus.

Two days later, the lead receives the next email.

---

### Email 3

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessEmail 3
Subject: How Did Everything Go?

Body:

Hi, Joe,

How was your workout?

If you thought the workouts were too hard, you can modify the example by performing 1/2 of the workout one day and the other half the next day. Over the next week, attempt to complete the entire workout in one session. Alternatively, you can still take me up on that offer for a free consultation by scheduling here.

If you felt the workouts were too easy or not challenging enough, you can perform the workout twice in one session and increase the number of rounds you complete per training session.

As promised, here is the free nutrition guide. Give it a read tonight (it’s only 10 pages), perform the workout routine again, and I will check-in tomorrow.

John Smith, NASM-CPT

Virtual Fitness Coach


 
The third email is again, delivered as promised. This email starts out with a question of concern, such as “How was your workout?” and immediately provides the two most common answers, easy or difficult, followed by a solution for each of those answers. The solution for those answers is the sales offer, which is located toward the beginning of the email. Finally, the next step is clearly laid out. The lead will hear from the fitness professional tomorrow after they have had an opportunity to read the nutrition guide as well as perform the bonus workout they just received.

The next day, the lead receives another email.

---

### Email 4

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessEmail 4
Subject: You Have the Tools. Do You Need A Guide?

Body:

Joe,

Over the past few days, your fitness toolbox has grown. First, you received a book that showed you how to design your own personalized home workouts. Then, we covered the most common questions and went over an example workout that you performed. Finally, we covered the basics of nutrition yesterday with your free nutrition guide. (If you have questions on that, please reply to this email and let’s cover them!) So, what’s next?

If you’re still slightly confused, nervous, or unsure, I’d like to offer you 20% off of my online fitness coaching. I only make this offer one time to all customers and only for 48 hours. Why, you ask? To help motivate you! Now is the time to make the changes you want to make. There is no reason to wait longer. If you’re serious about change, I want to help you out as much as possible.

You can click here to view all of the options I offer and to enroll in coaching. When you’re checking out, use the promo code JOE123 and you’ll receive your 20% off.

Don’t wait, Joe! Remember that this offer expires in 48 hours. I won’t e-mail you until after the offer expires, so make sure you enroll now if you’re interested.

John Smith, NASM-CPT

Virtual Fitness Coach


 
The fourth email starts off by reminding the lead of what they have already received and then offers a 20% discount on their first month of coaching. Finally, there is a sales opportunity, which is reasonable and not forceful. There is a limited amount of time to accept the offer and an explanation of why the offer expiration exists. The final line of the email states they will not hear from the fitness professional again until the offer expires.

Twenty-four hours after the promotional offer expires, the lead receives the final email in the sales workflow.

### Email 5

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessEmail 5
Subject: What Are Your Plans?

Body:

Joe,

It’s been 3 days since I emailed you about enrolling in coaching and you haven’t enrolled. Please tell me about your concerns or fears in enrolling. I understand that enrolling is a big step, but I’d love to help you make that step towards investing in your health. I’d like to make one final offer, and, after this, I promise I’ll stop sending these almost daily emails to you.

If you’re really interested in making a change in your life and achieving your goals, talk to me. Call me, text me, email me. My number is 312-867-5309. Contact me anytime. If I’m unable to answer, leave a voicemail and I’ll call you back. I promise that I will get back to you as soon as possible. We’re in this together and I’m on your team. There is no reason to go at it alone. Just send a simple text that says “Hi” and we’ll start from there.

John Smith, NASM-CPT

Virtual Fitness Coach


 
The final email begins by reminding the client that they missed the offer. They are then asked clearly what their concerns are for investing in coaching. This is followed by a call to action asking for their input which demonstrates the fitness professional is listening and wants to understand the lead’s perspective.

After the final email in the workflow, if the lead has not become a client, they return to being categorized as a prospect.

### Sales Email Techniques

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessSales Email TechniquesThere are many ways to structure and draft sales e-mails. The following sections will provide additional recommendations for sales e-mails, including uncovering needs, building rapport, and listening. Each of these techniques will help the fitness professional establish themselves as a credible expert in the virtual coaching arena.
Uncovering NeedsEach client has different needs. When marketing to a wide range of people in an automated process, the need being addressed must be comprehensive. The best need for sales e-mails to address can be uncovered through surveys and feedback from those leads that end up turning into clients. Existing clients are invaluable sales tools. Initially, the fitness professional will be using their knowledge of the industry and target market to develop their sales e-mails. However, after a successful client leaves their virtual coaching program, the fitness professional entrepreneur can survey former clients about what they found the most helpful about coaching. This feedback can be used to build a more accurate list of common needs virtual coaching can fulfill for clients. These needs can then be addressed in sales e-mails to demonstrate that the fitness professional understands the lead’s pain points.
To begin uncovering leads’ needs, the fitness professional can ask the following questions:


    What are the three most common goals training clients start with?
    What challenges did the client face that lead them to desire those goals?
    What is one way to demonstrate empathy for each of the challenges the client faced?


These questions can also be reworded and asked to former clients in an exit survey.

---

### Building Rapport

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 3The Onboarding ProcessBuilding Rapport Building rapport with consumers is not an easy task. Consumers largely distrust marketing, and this puts the fitness professional in a more difficult position as they attempt to build a long and meaningful relationship with their clients. The fitness professional will need to be positioned not only as an authority in the eyes of the consumer but also be perceived as genuine and authentic. Being genuine, honest, and truthful in the marketing content will build rapport with the consumer before they even contact the fitness professional or business.
The rapport a business builds with a consumer begins the instant the consumer becomes aware of the business. This awareness can come from many sources, including advertising, blogs, social media posts, and even word of mouth. Prior to obtaining a client’s e-mail address, a business must start to build a positive rapport with a prospect. This positive rapport can be established relatively easily by providing free, high-quality content used in marketing and then presenting an offer to the prospect in exchange for their e-mail address. Because of the rapport established during the marketing process, a prospect should have little objection to providing their contact information in exchange for higher-quality, higher-value content. The offer that is immediately presented to the newly created lead fulfills the promise made and further enhances positive rapport.
Throughout the entire sales email workflow, the fitness professional needs to demonstrate that they are an authority, genuine, and authentic throughout the sales email workflow. In addition, it should be obvious that they understand the issues the lead is facing and that the information being provided will start to solve those issues.
Sales Strategy Questions for Building Rapport
    How will the business position themselves as an authority by the second sales e-mail?
    How will authenticity be demonstrated and maintained throughout the sales process?
    What is one example of how a genuine concern for the lead’s issues will be demonstrated?

ListeningWith in-person training, the client and fitness professional have face-to-face interactions that allow for more opportunities to listen to a client’s needs, desires, and concerns. This face-to-face interaction is largely lost with virtual training. Interaction through e-mail, texting, and, to a lesser-extent, video conferencing do not provide the same opportunities for conversation that exist with in-person training. However, a fitness professional can overcome this obstacle in marketing and sales e-mails by preemptively addressing common objections, asking specific questions of the leads, as well as asking leads to reach out with any questions of their own.
Sales Strategy Questions for Listening
    What are three common objections to virtual coaching? What response could be given to those objections.
    What are three creative ways that leads can be encouraged to ask questions about coaching?
    What are the three most common questions leads and new clients ask?

---

### Billing and Collecting Payment

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 4Billing and Collecting PaymentBilling and Collecting PaymentAs with almost all aspects of a business, an efficient billing and payment processing system must aim to simplify things for the customer. A fitness business entrepreneur should strive for automation and repeatable processes and avoid needless redundancy. Billing and payment processing systems can seem overwhelming to those with non-technical backgrounds, but there are many affordable and less-technical options available on the market. This section will cover several points to consider when selecting a billing and payment processing system.
Selecting a Merchant ProcessorCompanies that provide billing services to businesses are called merchant processors or payment processors. These processors provide the business with simple solutions for accepting credit card payments on their website. Prior to a processor accepting a new business, the business will need at least three key items: a business license, an employer identification number, and a business checking account (Figure: Procure Processor).
Items Needed to Procure a Merchant or Billing ProcessorFigureProcure Processor
    Business license for the state in which the business was formed
    Federal Employer Identification Number (EIN) 
    
        The EIN functions much like a social security number for a business. An EIN can be applied for at no cost on the IRS website.
        Many companies offer to obtain an EIN number for a business for a fee, but this can easily be completed by the fitness professional. 
    
    
    Business checking account 
    
        The fitness professional must have a business checking account and not a consumer bank account in the business owner’s name. Depending on the type of business formed, this can have different tax consequences.
    
    

Note that not all of these are related to sales, but they are important items that every business needs.
Merchant processorsBusinesses that process credit card payments for another business.

---

### Key Things to Look for and Consider When Searching for a Payment Processor

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 4Billing and Collecting PaymentWhen comparing merchant processors, several different options should be evaluated. The one that provides features and services that best meet the needs of the fitness professional should be chosen. If the fitness professional is less technologically savvy, then a processor that offers simplicity when integrating with a website may be best. If the fitness professional requires advanced analytics, then they will need to ensure that they have a processor that offers integrations with various software applications and that exports sufficient types and quantities of data. Processors typically charge a small percentage of the sale that the business pays. These fees vary slightly depending on the processor or plan selected, but typically range from 2.6% to 3.2% + 30¢ per transaction.
Helpful HintUnless the business is generating millions of dollars in sales per year, price should not be a large consideration when looking for a processor. If a business performed $1 million in business over 5,000 transactions per year, the difference is $6,000 and may not be worth the time put into price shopping payment processors.
Key Things to Look for and Consider When Searching for a Payment Processor
    Fees ranging from 2.6% to 3.2% + 30¢ per transaction
    The ability to setup subscriptions and recurring client payments
    Daily deposits of funds
    Well-known and established company
    Ease of access to customer service

Once the payment processor is properly set up, the fitness professional entrepreneur can focus on setting up an automated billing and payment system for clients.
E-Commerce SystemWith a virtual coaching business, the business’s store is the company website. There is no physical location for a client to come into and ask questions. A business’s website is not only its marketing home but is also the company’s sales desk. A virtual coaching business needs to allow clients to purchase and immediately enroll in coaching, without face-to-face interaction with the fitness professional. A website that requires a potential client to wait for the fitness professional to contact them in order to enroll in coaching is increasing the steps and, subsequently, barriers to purchase. An e-commerce website should provide a clear path to virtual coaching, from basic information all the way to registration and payment.
Collecting Online PaymentsWith recurring payments, the business must collect payments monthly and on time. If payment is collected too early, a client's budget may not allow the payment to process. If payment is collected too late, a client may be receiving free coaching, even if they do not intend to continue. Having consistent, automated billing also allows for the fitness professional to have predictable and consistent revenue coming into the business. Allowing a client to enter their own billing information into a website and subscribe to coaching via an online e-commerce platform integrated with the business’s merchant processor will allow payments and subscription fees to be automated. Sending out individual invoices to clients every month is time consuming and allows for potential errors in amounts billed and consistency in payments. Automated billing and payment processing take stress away from the fitness professional, allowing them to focus on more impactful activities such as content development, programming, and coaching.

---

### Summary

Chapter 8Selling Virtual CoachingLesson 4Billing and Collecting PaymentSummaryLike any small business owner, a fitness professional who owns a virtual coaching business has roles and responsibilities that extend beyond developing programs and coaching clients, such as marketing their services and generating sales. Sales are vital to a company’s success. Without sales, there is no revenue and without revenue, there is no company. While focusing on sales is often viewed with a negative connotation, it does not have to be. Ethical sales strategies and techniques are rooted in educating and helping others. It, ultimately, comes down to identifying potential clients’ needs, offering solutions to those needs, and then asking them to make a financial commitment for those solutions. The aspects of running a business that can be automated should be, as automation frees up time to focus on areas of the business that cannot be and are vital to establishing authority, credibility, and authenticity in the market, such as content development, programming, and coaching and serving clients.
Marketing and sales processes can largely be automated and when combined with proper market segmentation, allows for personalized messaging delivered directly into the hands, and eyes, of the business’s target audience. Automated sales workflows leveraging e-mail can be customized to not only deliver highly impactful content to potential clients but can also be used to contact existing customers about service add-ons and upgrades and re-engage former clients in an attempt to build another meaningful relationship. Please refer to the video below for a summary of the topics discussed within this chapter.

### Online Marketing Vehicles

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesOnline Marketing Vehicles After defining the customer avatar, the fitness professional is ready to determine the best online marketing vehicle for their business. Using information from the customer avatar can guide the fitness professional to the best marketing vehicle to reach the maximum number of potential clients. Online marketing vehicles include social media, email, and websites.
13_Phunkod/Shutterstock.com

---

### Social Media

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesSocial Media According to the Pew Research Center (2019), nearly 72% of all Americans use at least some form of social media (Figure: Social Media). With the volume of potential clients using social media, it is an integral part of marketing and should not be underestimated. Social media can be a great way to bring awareness to a fitness professional’s services and even serve to drive business.
FigureSocial Media

---

### Variation Among Social Media Platforms

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesVariation Among Social Media PlatformsNote that not all social media platforms serve the same audience. There are many social media options available for consumers and, as a result, it can be difficult to know where to start in order to reach the target audience. This is where knowing the customer avatar is critical. Different social media platforms tend to have different populations as their most frequent users.
According to recent statistics, the number one demographic for Facebook users worldwide are men and women ages 25 to 34 (Clement, 2020). Contrast that to the younger demographic, the top three social media platforms for users ages 13 to 17 are YouTube, Instagram, and Snapchat (Figure: Social Media Users). Knowing this information will inform fitness professionals about where to find their target audience and how to vary the messages accordingly.
FigureSocial Media Users

---

### Email

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesEmail Email marketing is a proven form of digital marketing. Potential clients who provide fitness professionals with their email have demonstrated some form of interest in the product or service. Some common opt-ins may include newsletter subscriptions, instructional e-books, or PDFs. The email content that is offered must be compelling enough for someone to provide their email address. This can prove to be difficult, as individuals are increasingly more protective over their personal information.
WebsiteLike a storefront, a website is often one of the first impressions potential customers receive about an online business. Many consumers make their purchasing decisions based on the clarity and structure of a business's website. There are numerous services available when deciding how to host a website. WordPress, Squarespace, and Wix are some of the more popular options. Here a couple of things to remember when potential customers go to a website:

    Is the website easy to navigate?
    Is it clear what the company is selling?
    Is there an evergreen or deadline offer clearly being displayed?
    Are next steps to purchasing clear (i.e., scheduling an assessment or buying a program)?

---

### Online Advertising for Small Businesses

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesOnline Advertising for Small BusinessesOnline advertising can be an effective way to boost traffic to a website and increase the potential for conversion of prospects into paid customers. When it comes to online advertising, there are multiple digital platforms that can be used successfully.
Social Media PlatformsSocial media platforms such as Instagram and Facebook offer paid advertising to small businesses. Using these platforms for advertising allows a fitness professional to target the demographic they are marketing to. For example, a paid advertisement can target Facebook users by geographic location, age, and even other Facebook pages liked.
Search and Review PlatformsAnother way to advertise for a small business is by utilizing search platforms such as Google or user review sites like Yelp. These platforms are commonly used by consumers who are searching for a specific service or product. These platforms typically utilize a pay-per-click model of paid advertising. An advantage to using platforms of this type is that the user searching for the service is already at least somewhat interested.
ProspectA person whom the business has identified as fitting their client criteria but has not yet provided their information.
Pay-per-click modelA model of digital marketing in which the advertiser pays a fee every time their ad is clicked.

---

### Online Marketing Best Practices and Professionalism

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesOnline Marketing Best Practices and ProfessionalismTo truly be a successful online marketer, a fitness professional must know more than the various digital marketing platforms. They must also have a strong knowledge of which methods are most effective. The following section will discuss and review those methods and provide specific examples of implementation strategies.
Search Engine OptimizationOn any given day, there are over 2 million searches conducted on Google alone (Patel, 2019). This is why search engine optimization, or SEO, has become an important factor in marketing and searchability. SEO is a method of improving a website's ranking during organic, unpaid searches. Research shows the first five results on Google get 67% of the clicks (Patel, 2019). In such a noisy, online world, ranking high on the list of these searches is paramount. One of the best and most effective ways to increase SEO is by producing quality content. Items such as blog posts and e-books can organically drive people to the site, which will, in turn, increase overall ranking in the eyes of the search engine.
Engagement Methods for Digital MarketingTo produce engaging content, a fitness professional should view their actions strategically, rather than use a one-size-fits-all approach. When thinking about posting a piece of content, a marketer needs a system to determine the effectiveness of their post. The fitness professional must set clear metrics of success for their digital marketing efforts and campaigns. These metrics should align with their business goals. Fitness professionals can use analytics tools such as Google Analytics and Sprout Social to monitor what is and what is not working and know how to adjust accordingly.
Bloomicon/Shutterstock.comSearch engine optimizationA method of improving a website's ranking during organic, unpaid searches.
OrganicTraffic to a website or account that is not paid for.

---

### Content

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesThere are many methods for delivering online messages but most fall into one of the four quadrants of digital marketing. These quadrants include building authority, social proof, connection, and attention (Figure: Four Quadrants of Digital Marketing).
FigureFour Quadrants of Digital Market

---

### Webinars

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesWebinarsWebinars are one of the fastest-growing digital marketing methods. Webinars are live or prerecorded virtual conferences or presentations. Webinars can help establish fitness professionals as an authority in a specific topic or niche. For example, if a nutrition coach is well-versed in weight loss, he or she could host a webinar on the topic of intermittent fasting.
Webinar StructureThere are typically two types of webinars that can be run—paid and free. Although a paid webinar can make money, it can also potentially limit the number of people who are able to attend. On the other hand, hosting a webinar may aid in lead qualification as only the most serious potential clients will be interested in attending. One possible strategy when hosting webinars is to host it for a small fee and then reveal a back-end offer near the end of the webinar. That offer could be a bit more exclusive for clients who are interested in learning more about the topic or possibly purchasing a higher-end service. Using that same example of the nutrition coach, a potential strategy could be to offer a nutrition consultation or a meal plan to anyone who attended the webinar. Web-based services like Zoom and Webinar Ninja allow any fitness professional to easily host a webinar.
fizkes/Shutterstock.comBack-end offerAn additional offer to a customer who has already purchased a product or service from the business, also referred to as an upsell.

---

### Social Media Posts

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesSocial Media Posts Because each social media platform caters to a different audience, it is wise to post on multiple platforms rather than posting on just one. Posting on multiple platforms has been shown to have a positive impact on brand loyalty (Çiçek & Erdoğmuş, 2012). Considering this, the question then becomes, “What to post?” Fitness professionals should consider the four digital marketing quadrants when determining what type of content to post (Figure: Examples of Four Quadrants).
FigureExamples of Four QuadrantsIt is best practice to use messaging in each quadrant from time to time so that social media marketing efforts are well-rounded and, therefore, more effective.

---

### Social Media Schedule

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesSocial Media Schedule Developing a social media schedule is critical to maintaining consistency with posting. Posting consistently is difficult but it becomes more so when there is no plan in place. Software like Later or Sprout Social can also aid in developing a social media schedule. These resources can provide analytics, such as peak engagement times for various social media platforms, that will help the fitness professional develop a deeper understanding of their audience.

When developing a social media plan, there are several factors to consider:


    Which days receive the highest engagement?
    What type of content resonates most with the audience?
    What events or deadlines are being marketed toward?

Sammby/Shutterstock.comGetting TechnicalThere are many ways for fitness professionals to drive organic traffic to their social media pages.


    On Instagram, post stories of clients' successes (text message, videos, pics, etc.) and encourage them to share the post. In this scenario, the fitness professional not only reaches their audience but their client’s network/audience as well.
    On Facebook, Twitter, or Instagram, the fitness professional can tag larger fitness accounts in the posts. If the larger accounts repost it, the fitness professional’s organic reach will increase.
    Facebook Live and Instagram Live are popular tools used by fitness professionals. For example, collaborating with other fitness professionals in live Q&A sessions is a great way to drive traffic to social media pages or websites.

---

### Email Campaigns

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesEmail Campaigns An email list is crucial for marketing to an already interested audience. Remember, a person who has willingly given their e-mail is already interested in a service, at least on some level, which makes them more likely to convert into clients. When considering which email service to use, the fitness professional should find one that is reliable and supports them in executing their marketing plan. Some of the most popular email marketing options include Mailchimp, Constant Contact, and ConvertKit.
stscheb/Shutterstock.comCreating an Email Opt-InFigureEmail Opt-InIt is one thing to have an e-mail list, but it is another to actually grow it. This is where an opt-in email becomes useful. An opt-in is when a consumer provides their e-mail address, usually in exchange for some sort of free content. These opt-ins are often in the form of pop-ups that appear when a person navigates through a website. Some common opt-in offers include newsletter subscriptions, instructional e-books, PDFs, or free training programs.
The quality of the opt-in should be high. The key is to provide enough value for the potential customer, while also giving them some insight into what purchasing a service might be like. Here is an example of a potential e-mail opt-in (Figure: Email Opt-In). Once that person's email is collected, they become a subscriber and a welcome email is typically sent confirming their subscription and delivering the promised content. From that point on, the fitness professional should continue to deliver valuable information to their subscribers so that they feel connected and part of the online community.
Newsletters Newsletters are a great way to keep an audience engaged. Think of a newsletter as an online newspaper for a business. It keeps customers, subscribers, and potential customers up to date with current business events. Newsletters are where many fitness professionals highlight clients and tell their subscribers about any upcoming specials or events. Typically, newsletters fall into the Connection category of the four quadrants of virtual marketing.
This Is Me/Shutterstock.comOpt-in emailAn option to join an e-mail list.

---

### YouTube

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesYouTubeWith over 2 billion users worldwide, YouTube is one of the most popular forms of media for consumer video consumption. Many online fitness professionals have gained popularity by dedicating their time to developing a YouTube platform. Creating a YouTube channel is a great way for a fitness professional to build authority in their respective field. A study examining student engagement while watching videos showed that shorter videos are more engaging than longer videos (Guo et al., 2014, p. 49). The study recommended video segments shorter than 6 minutes. As people have access to more content, holding viewer attention becomes an ever-present challenge. One method is to select or create an engaging thumbnail for all YouTube videos. An engaging thumbnail helps a video stand out as people scroll through the numerous YouTube fitness videos available.
ART PAL/Shutterstock.comBlogsBlogs are one of the best ways to create authoritative content and drive traffic to a website. Additionally, traffic created from blogs helps improve website visibility, search engine ranking, and ultimately SEO.
Methods for Increasing Blog Visibility In the early inception of blogging, visibility was a relatively easy thing to obtain. However, as more people have started blogging, visibility has become much harder to achieve. By some estimates, there are over 600 million blogs on the Internet today (Petrov, 2020). With the large number of blogs available, the question then becomes, “How does the fitness professional’s blog stand out?”
Vasyl Nagernyak/Shutterstock.comHere are a few methods to gain greater visibility through blogging:


    Consistency – To gain a loyal audience, a blogger must post consistently. Try setting a blog-release schedule.
    Timing – Publish blog posts on high-traffic social media days. For example, releasing a blog on a Tuesday, normally a high-traffic day, will likely yield greater engagement than a blog posted on a Saturday.
    Naming a Post – All digital marketing vies for consumer attention. The title of a blog post is the first thing someone sees and will often determine whether they choose to read on. Titles that begin with the phrases “How to…” and “Here are 5 ways to” tend to get more traffic over other more general titles.
    Collaborations – Collaborating with other fitness professionals is a great way to increase the reach of a blog post. Interviews and guest posts are all methods that increase the shareability of a blog post. The post then gains exposure for not only the fitness professional’s followers but also to the followers of the other collaborator.

---

### Proper Messaging

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesProper MessagingMessaging varies depending on the social media platform and on the message being delivered. Generally, it is important to keep messaging concise and direct. Whenever posting digital content, proper messaging and professionalism is crucial. This is true for both professional and personal social media accounts as a fitness professional is ultimately a direct representation of their business. Proper grammar, spelling, and punctuation should be used in all posts. Additionally, most posts should be done in an authoritative tone to convey the expertise of the fitness professional.
Proper Use of Visuals As the use of social media and digital marketing has grown, it is more important now than ever for the fitness professional to incorporate quality visuals into their social media marketing. The standard has been raised as more people become adept at visuals and social media. Camera phones have made this easier. With most phones now equipped with high-quality cameras, it is often unnecessary to hire a photographer to take professional photos. In the past, the creation of graphics required expensive software with a steep learning curve. However, now there are many graphic software products available, such as Canva or PicMonkey that make it easier and more cost-effective (and in many cases free) to design and edit high-quality marketing material.
Tero Vesalainen/Shutterstock.comIn addition to the quality of the visuals, the formatting of video and pictures is also important. For example, consider recording an exercise video with the intent of posting it on multiple video media platforms. Shooting the video in portrait mode (vertical orientation) is great for posting on an Instagram feed but it is not the ideal camera orientation for posting videos on YouTube. Landscape mode (horizontal orientation) is the preferred method for posting videos on YouTube and Facebook (Figure: Video Format). Videos shot in portrait mode leave extra space in the margins of the video when viewed on a widescreen, which can be distracting to the viewer.
FigureVideo Format

---

### Word-of-Mouth Marketing

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesWord-of-Mouth Marketing Despite the advancements in technology and digital platforms, word-of-mouth marketing continues to be an incredibly effective form of marketing. According to research, 67% of consumers are based on word of mouth (Karlíček et al., 2010). As a fitness professional, developing trust and a strong referral network from current clients is a good way to grow a sustainable online business.
Referrals Offering existing members some type of incentive may encourage referrals. Ideally, clients will refer potential customers regardless of an incentive, but offering one can certainly help. One way to incentivize referrals is to offer a gift card or credit to clients who refer new clients to a business. A potential benefit of offering gift cards, as opposed to discounting an existing client's service, is that money spent on gift cards could potentially be written off for tax purposes, depending on the business situation. Additionally, gift cards do not devalue the price of training options.
CoCoArt_Ua/Shutterstock.com

---

### Testimonials

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 2Online Marketing Vehicles and StrategiesTestimonialsTestimonials tap into a powerful, scientific marketing principle—the principle of social proof. The principle of social proof suggests that individuals observe and assume the behaviors and actions of others to determine what is correct or to find out what other people think is correct (Cialdini, 2006). If a potential customer sees that someone like them has had good results and a positive experience, they are more likely to choose that same service over another one. 
Obtaining TestimonialsBoth written and video testimonials are excellent pieces of marketing material. There is some debate over which method is more effective. Luckily, with transcription services readily available, obtaining both is easy and relatively inexpensive. One way to obtain both types is to record a video testimonial of a client’s experience and then send the audio file to a transcription service to have it converted into written format.
When gathering testimonials from clients, fitness professionals should provide a guide to follow so results come out clean and succinct. This guide may include recommendations for talking points and sample questions. The following are examples of questions that may yield a strong testimonial:


    What was your experience when you first started working with (insert name of fitness professional and business)?
    What was the biggest challenge you faced?
    What is your experience like now?
    What is your favorite thing about training with (insert name of business)?


Questions like these create a story or a hero’s arch for the client testimonial. These questions relate their struggles and connect their story to others and potential clients who will likely share some of the same challenges.
Helpful HintNot sure whether to have a client do a written or a video testimonial? Why not do both?

With online transcription services, fitness professionals can record the video and then send it to a transcription company to have it converted into text. Typically, these companies charge a per-minute fee, and, for a short video, it is a valuable time saver. Once the fitness professional has the transcription and video, they can easily post and share them on various platforms.
MerchandiseMerchandise is often an underrated marketing tool. Merchandise is anything that clients can use or wear that represents a brand. Merchandise transforms the client into a walking billboard. Examples of effective merchandise for fitness businesses include t-shirts, stickers, and protein shaker bottles. The key to any sort of merchandise is to make sure the brand is clear and visible. Merchandise can also be an effective way to increase client retention. For example, if a client reaches a certain weight-loss goal, a great strategy would be to send them a t-shirt for their success and have them post a picture of themselves wearing it. This not only increases client retention but also helps build social proof for the brand.

---

### Calculating Marketing ROI

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 3Evaluating Marketing EffortsCalculating Marketing ROI Calculating marketing Return On Investment (ROI) is a method for determining the effectiveness of a marketing campaign. The simplest equation to calculating ROI is as follows (Figure: Return on Investment):
FigureReturn on InvestmentReturn on investment (ROI)A performance measure used to evaluate the efficiency of an investment or compare the efficiency of a number of different investments.

---

### Content

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 3Evaluating Marketing EffortsFor example, imagine a nutrition coach is running a marketing campaign to promote their one-on-one meal planning service. The marketing campaign costs $200 and they have seen a $1,000 increase in revenue for that service. To calculate the return on the campaign investment, the fitness professional would subtract the cost from the sales growth and then divide that number by the costs associated with their marketing campaign (Figure: Example Return on Investment). 
FigureExample Return on InvestmentA positive return on investment signifies the returns were greater than the cost. A negative return on investment signifies the total costs associated with the marketing effort were greater than the returns.

---

### Limitations of ROI

Chapter 9Marketing Virtual Coaching Lesson 3Evaluating Marketing EffortsLimitations of ROI Not all marketing efforts are afforded an immediate return on investment. A fitness professional may host a webinar in June and then be contacted by a potential client in November for online training. It can often be difficult to pinpoint if that person came in because of a particular marketing effort. Always expecting immediate ROI from a marketing effort can also be problematic. Some marketing efforts need time to take effect. In general, while it is important to understand a campaign’s ROI, it may be more ideal to have a long-term mindset when it comes to determining the true value and success of marketing efforts. 
SummaryMarketing a virtual coaching business can seem like a complex and even daunting task. However, as a fitness professional, it is necessary to ensure the growth and success of an online business. This is especially true in the current landscape with demand for fitness services increasing and consumer desires and purchasing habits evolving. With proper marketing planning, knowledge of the various marketing vehicles, and an understanding of the best methods to increase visibility and engagement, a thriving and sustainable online fitness business is within reach.
